Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. It sounds very clear, like someone standing right beside me, even when the unit is quiet... and nobody else seems to hear it. It’s more that they tell me which patient records have been changed. Like it comes through the call-light speaker or the vents, very clear, and I can understand it. They’ll come through the call-light speaker or the hallway vents and tell me which patient records have been changed. I don’t really have trouble understanding the words. I just… I know nobody else seems to hear it, so I’m trying not to make too much of it.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices ever tell you to do something or take a specific action?\n\nPatient: Not really like… “go do this now,” no. It’s more that they tell me which patient records have been changed. Like it comes through the call-light speaker or the vents, very clear, and I can understand it. I know that probably sounds strange, but it’s not like they’re ordering me around exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really depressed, no. I mean, I’ve been stressed and a little embarrassed about all this, but I wouldn’t say I’ve been feeling sad exactly. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to keep your thoughts straight or express them in a way that feels coherent to you?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… but I don’t want to overstate it. It’s more like my mind keeps jumping around — from wound measurements, to patient initials, to dates on the forms, to what it all means — like I have ten tabs open at once and none of them will close.  \n\nI can usually explain it if I slow down, I think… I hope I’m making sense right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are disconnected from each other in a way that feels unusual or difficult for you to manage?\n\nPatient: Yes… somewhat. I don’t know if I’d call them disconnected exactly, but they move too fast and jump around. Like I’ll be thinking about wound measurements, then patient initials, then dates on the forms, then what it all means, and it’s like I’ve got ten tabs open in my head and none of them will close.\n\nI can usually follow it if I really slow myself down, but it does feel hard to manage sometimes. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you felt extremely energetic or irritable and didn't need much sleep?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, there are days where I feel like I’m finally awake, like I can see what needs to be fixed at work and I’m supposed to do something about it.\n\nI don’t always think of it as a bad thing at first. It feels productive, kind of important. But if my husband, or one of the nurses, tells me to slow down, I can get really irritated… I snap more than I should, like they’re getting in the way of something that matters. I know that probably doesn’t sound great.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I… yes, I guess it does feel like it’s about my role at work. Not in a dramatic way, I don’t mean to sound important or anything.

It’s like they’re coming from the call-light speaker, or sometimes the hallway vents, telling me which patient records have been changed. It sounds very clear, like someone standing right beside me, even when the unit is quiet... and nobody else seems to hear it. I just thought I should come in and make sure I’m handling it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to understand what the voices are telling you, or does it make perfect sense to you?\n\nPatient: It makes sense to me, mostly. I mean… I know it probably sounds odd, but the voices are very clear, like someone right next to me, not muffled or anything. They’ll come through the call-light speaker or the hallway vents and tell me which patient records have been changed. I don’t really have trouble understanding the words. I just… I know nobody else seems to hear it, so I’m trying not to make too much of it.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices ever tell you to do something or take a specific action?\n\nPatient: Not really like… “go do this now,” no. It’s more that they tell me which patient records have been changed. Like it comes through the call-light speaker or the vents, very clear, and I can understand it. I know that probably sounds strange, but it’s not like they’re ordering me around exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really depressed, no. I mean, I’ve been stressed and a little embarrassed about all this, but I wouldn’t say I’ve been feeling sad exactly. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to keep your thoughts straight or express them in a way that feels coherent to you?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… but I don’t want to overstate it. It’s more like my mind keeps jumping around — from wound measurements, to patient initials, to dates on the forms, to what it all means — like I have ten tabs open at once and none of them will close.  \n\nI can usually explain it if I slow down, I think… I hope I’m making sense right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are disconnected from each other in a way that feels unusual or difficult for you to manage?\n\nPatient: Yes… somewhat. I don’t know if I’d call them disconnected exactly, but they move too fast and jump around. Like I’ll be thinking about wound measurements, then patient initials, then dates on the forms, then what it all means, and it’s like I’ve got ten tabs open in my head and none of them will close.\n\nI can usually follow it if I really slow myself down, but it does feel hard to manage sometimes. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you felt extremely energetic or irritable and didn't need much sleep?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, there are days where I feel like I’m finally awake, like I can see what needs to be fixed at work and I’m supposed to do something about it.\n\nI don’t always think of it as a bad thing at first. I try not to make too much of it because at first it feels like I’m just being useful, like I’m finally awake and seeing what needs to be fixed at work.\n\nBut then if my husband, or one of the nurses, tells me to slow down, I can get… sharper than I mean to. I don’t know if I’d call it a “mission”… that sounds a little big, and I’m not trying to make myself sound special.

But yes, I do feel like I noticed something in the wound-care charts that other people aren’t taking seriously. There’s a pattern there, and when management says it’s just late entries or coding mistakes, I try to hear that, I really do… but it doesn’t feel right.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well... I’m a little embarrassed to say it, because I know it may sound strange, but I’ve been hearing voices at work. It’s like they’re coming from the call-light speaker, or sometimes the hallway vents, telling me which patient records have been changed. It sounds very clear, like someone standing right beside me, even when the unit is quiet... and nobody else seems to hear it. I just thought I should come in and make sure I’m handling it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to understand what the voices are telling you, or does it make perfect sense to you?\n\nPatient: It makes sense to me, mostly. I mean… I know it probably sounds odd, but the voices are very clear, like someone right next to me, not muffled or anything. They’ll come through the call-light speaker or the hallway vents and tell me which patient records have been changed. I don’t really have trouble understanding the words. I just… I know nobody else seems to hear it, so I’m trying not to make too much of it.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices ever tell you to do something or take a specific action?\n\nPatient: Not really like… “go do this now,” no. It’s more that they tell me which patient records have been changed. Like it comes through the call-light speaker or the vents, very clear, and I can understand it. I know that probably sounds strange, but it’s not like they’re ordering me around exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really depressed, no. I mean, I’ve been stressed and a little embarrassed about all this, but I wouldn’t say I’ve been feeling sad exactly. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to keep your thoughts straight or express them in a way that feels coherent to you?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… but I don’t want to overstate it. It’s more like my mind keeps jumping around — from wound measurements, to patient initials, to dates on the forms, to what it all means — like I have ten tabs open at once and none of them will close.  \n\nI can usually explain it if I slow down, I think… I hope I’m making sense right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are disconnected from each other in a way that feels unusual or difficult for you to manage?\n\nPatient: Yes… somewhat. I don’t know if I’d call them disconnected exactly, but they move too fast and jump around. Like I’ll be thinking about wound measurements, then patient initials, then dates on the forms, then what it all means, and it’s like I’ve got ten tabs open in my head and none of them will close.\n\nI can usually follow it if I really slow myself down, but it does feel hard to manage sometimes. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you felt extremely energetic or irritable and didn't need much sleep?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, there are days where I feel like I’m finally awake, like I can see what needs to be fixed at work and I’m supposed to do something about it.\n\nI don’t always think of it as a bad thing at first. It feels productive, kind of important. But if my husband, or one of the nurses, tells me to slow down, I can get really irritated… I snap more than I should, like they’re getting in the way of something that matters. I know that probably doesn’t sound great.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voices are real or do you think they might be a product of your own mind?\n\nPatient: I… I want to say maybe it’s stress or my mind trying to make sense of things, because I know that sounds more reasonable. But when it happens, it feels real. Really real.\n\nI hear it from the call-light speaker sometimes, or from the hallway vents, telling me which patient records have been changed. It’s clear, like somebody is standing right next to me saying it. And then the unit will be quiet, and nobody else seems to hear anything, so… I know that makes me sound… I don’t know.\n\nI guess part of me thinks it must be my mind, because no one else hears it. But in the moment, it doesn’t feel like it’s coming from me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely energetic or irritable that lasted for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: Yes… it has lasted more than just a day sometimes. I try not to make too much of it because at first it feels like I’m just being useful, like I’m finally awake and seeing what needs to be fixed at work.\n\nBut then if my husband, or one of the nurses, tells me to slow down, I can get… sharper than I mean to. I snap at them, like they’re blocking something important. I don’t like admitting that, because I know it doesn’t sound very good.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel irritable most of the time or just during certain periods?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s all the time, no. Most of the time I’m… I try to be pretty even, or at least I hope I am.\n\nIt’s more like certain periods, certain days, where I feel like I’m finally awake and I can see everything that needs fixing at work. And then if my husband or one of the nurses tells me to slow down, I can snap at them. Like they’re getting in the way of something important.\n\nI know that probably sounds harsh. I don’t mean to be that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voices or messages you're hearing are specifically about you or your role at work?\n\nPatient: I… yes, I guess it does feel like it’s about my role at work. Not in a dramatic way, I don’t mean to sound important or anything.\n\nIt’s just… the wound-care charts, there’s a pattern in them. I know management doesn’t want people seeing it. My supervisor says it’s just late entries or coding mistakes, and I try to take that in, I really do, but it doesn’t feel like that to me.\n\nIt feels like they’re watching what I write because I’m the one who caught it. Does that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe you have a special role or mission at work that others don't understand?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a “mission”… that sounds a little big, and I’m not trying to make myself sound special.\n\nBut yes, I do feel like I noticed something in the wound-care charts that other people aren’t taking seriously. There’s a pattern there, and when management says it’s just late entries or coding mistakes, I try to hear that, I really do… but it doesn’t feel right.\n\nIt feels like they don’t want anyone to see it, and like they’re watching what I write because I’m the one who caught it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have special insights or abilities at work that set you apart from your colleagues?\n\nPatient: Maybe… yes, a little. I don’t mean like I’m better than the other aides or nurses. They’re good at their jobs.\n\nIt’s just with those wound-care charts, I can see a pattern that other people keep brushing off. My supervisor says it’s late entries or coding mistakes, and I try to accept that, I really do. But it feels too connected to just be mistakes.\n\nAnd I know this may sound odd, but it feels like management doesn’t want anyone to notice it… and like they’re watching what I write because I’m the one who caught it. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I wouldn’t say it makes me more important than anyone else. I don’t want to come across like that. Everyone works hard, and I know I’m just one aide there.

But… I do feel like I’m the one who saw it. The wound-care charts are showing a pattern, and management doesn’t want anyone to see it. My supervisor keeps saying it’s just late entries or coding mistakes, and I try to accept that because I don’t want to be difficult.

But I can tell they’re watching what I write now, because I’m the one who caught it. I know that sounds like a lot… I’m just trying to explain it the right way.

I snap at them, like they’re blocking something important. I know that sounds like a lot… I’m just trying to explain it the right way.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think others would see the same pattern in the wound-care charts if they looked closely?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 50 / nursing_assistant / some_college\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I know the wound-care charts are showing a pattern management doesn't want anyone to see, and even when my supervisor says it's just late entries or coding mistakes, I can tell they're watching what I write because I'm the one who caught it.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I think… yes, if they really looked closely, they would see it too. I’m not saying I’m the only person who could ever notice it or anything like that.

It’s just that once you see how the wound-care notes line up, it’s hard to unsee. My supervisor keeps saying it’s late entries or coding mistakes, and I’m trying not to be stubborn about it, but it doesn’t feel like that to me.

And I know this probably sounds suspicious, but it feels like management doesn’t want people looking too closely. Since I brought it up, I can tell they’re paying attention to what I write. I hope that makes sense… I’m not trying to sound dramatic.

I don’t know if I’d call them disconnected exactly, but they move too fast and jump around. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you felt extremely energetic or irritable and didn't need much sleep?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I know that probably doesn’t sound great.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voices are real or do you think they might be a product of your own mind?\n\nPatient: I… I want to say maybe it’s stress or my mind trying to make sense of things, because I know that sounds more reasonable. I snap at them, like they’re blocking something important. I don’t mean to be that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voices or messages you're hearing are specifically about you or your role at work?\n\nPatient: I… yes, I guess it does feel like it’s about my role at work. My supervisor says it’s just late entries or coding mistakes, and I try to take that in, I really do, but it doesn’t feel like that to me.\n\nIt feels like they’re watching what I write because I’m the one who caught it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have special insights or abilities at work that set you apart from your colleagues?\n\nPatient: Maybe… yes, a little. No… I don’t think it makes me better than anyone. I really don’t want you to think I’m saying that.

I just feel like I noticed something important in the wound-care charts that other people are explaining away. one who caught it.\n- key: S020\n  name: Hallucinations\n  description: Perception-like experiences that occur without an external stimulus and are as vivid and clear as normal perception.\n  manifestation: I'm here because I keep hearing voices from the call-light speaker and the hallway vents telling me which patient records have been changed, clear as somebody standing next to me, even when the unit is quiet and nobody else hears it.\n- key: S033\n  name: Elevated_Irritable_Mood\n  description: A distinct period of abnormally and persistently elevated, expansive, or irritable mood.\n  manifestation: Some days I feel like I'm finally awake and meant to fix everything at work, but if my husband or a nurse tells me to slow down I snap at them like they're blocking something important.\n- key: S034\n  name: Increased_Activity\n  description: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.\n  manifestation: I have so much energy that after a full shift I come home and clean, rewrite notes, sort supplies, and pace around the kitchen while my husband is begging me to sit down.\n- key: S037\n  name: Pressured_Speech\n  description: More talkative than usual or pressure to keep talking.\n  manifestation: When I start explaining the documentation problem I can hear myself talking faster and faster, but I can't stop because if I pause I feel like I'll lose the whole thread.\n- key: S038\n  name: Racing_Thoughts\n  description: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.\n  manifestation: My mind keeps jumping from wound measurements to patient initials to dates on the forms to what it all means, like ten tabs are open at once and none of them will close.\n- key: S040\n  name: Increased_Goal_Activity\n  description: Increase in goal-directed activity (at work, school, socially, or sexually) or purposeless psychomotor agitation.\n  manifestation: I've been staying after shifts, calling coworkers on their days off, and making new binders and checklists for the rollout because it feels urgent that I get the whole facility corrected before someone gets hurt.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the manic episode\n  manifestation: I never heard the call-light voices or saw hidden messages in the charts before I feel like my work reputation and my plans for LPN school are falling apart because of this.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 2 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about two months, since I was asked to help lead the wound-care documentation rollout.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 50 / nursing_assistant / some_college\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S020\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you feel like your ability to see patterns in the wound-care charts makes you more important or special compared to others at work?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it makes me more important than anyone else. The wound-care charts are showing a pattern, and management doesn’t want anyone to see it. My supervisor keeps saying it’s just late entries or coding mistakes, and I try to accept that because I don’t want to be difficult.\n\nBut I can tell they’re watching what I write now, because I’m the one who caught it. I mean… I know it probably sounds odd, but the voices are very clear, like someone right next to me, not muffled or anything. They’ll come through the call-light speaker or the hallway vents and tell me which patient records have been changed. I don’t really have trouble understanding the words. I just… I know nobody else seems to hear it, so I’m trying not to make too much of it.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices ever tell you to do something or take a specific action?\n\nPatient: Not really like… “go do this now,” no. It’s more that they tell me which patient records have been changed. Like it comes through the call-light speaker or the vents, very clear, and I can understand it. I mean, I’ve been stressed and a little embarrassed about all this, but I wouldn’t say I’ve been feeling sad exactly. It’s more like my mind keeps jumping around — from wound measurements, to patient initials, to dates on the forms, to what it all means — like I have ten tabs open at once and none of them will close.  \n\nI can usually explain it if I slow down, I think… I hope I’m making sense right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are disconnected from each other in a way that feels unusual or difficult for you to manage?\n\nPatient: Yes… somewhat. I don’t know if I’d call them disconnected exactly, but they move too fast and jump around. Like I’ll be thinking about wound measurements, then patient initials, then dates on the forms, then what it all means, and it’s like I’ve got ten tabs open in my head and none of them will close.\n\nI can usually follow it if I really slow myself down, but it does feel hard to manage sometimes. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you felt extremely energetic or irritable and didn't need much sleep?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, there are days where I feel like I’m finally awake, like I can see what needs to be fixed at work and I’m supposed to do something about it.\n\nI don’t always think of it as a bad thing at first. It feels productive, kind of important. But if my husband, or one of the nurses, tells me to slow down, I can get really irritated… I snap more than I should, like they’re getting in the way of something that matters. I know that probably doesn’t sound great.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voices are real or do you think they might be a product of your own mind?\n\nPatient: I… I want to say maybe it’s stress or my mind trying to make sense of things, because I know that sounds more reasonable. But when it happens, it feels real. Really real.\n\nI hear it from the call-light speaker sometimes, or from the hallway vents, telling me which patient records have been changed. It’s clear, like somebody is standing right next to me saying it. And then the unit will be quiet, and nobody else seems to hear anything, so… I know that makes me sound… I don’t know.\n\nI guess part of me thinks it must be my mind, because no one else hears it. But in the moment, it doesn’t feel like it’s coming from me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely energetic or irritable that lasted for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: Yes… it has lasted more than just a day sometimes. I try not to make too much of it because at first it feels like I’m just being useful, like I’m finally awake and seeing what needs to be fixed at work.\n\nBut then if my husband, or one of the nurses, tells me to slow down, I can get… sharper than I mean to. I snap at them, like they’re blocking something important. I don’t like admitting that, because I know it doesn’t sound very good.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel irritable most of the time or just during certain periods?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s all the time, no. Most of the time I’m… I try to be pretty even, or at least I hope I am.\n\nIt’s more like certain periods, certain days, where I feel like I’m finally awake and I can see everything that needs fixing at work. And then if my husband or one of the nurses tells me to slow down, I can snap at them. Like they’re getting in the way of something important.\n\nI know that probably sounds harsh. I don’t mean to be that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voices or messages you're hearing are specifically about you or your role at work?\n\nPatient: I… yes, I guess it does feel like it’s about my role at work. Not in a dramatic way, I don’t mean to sound important or anything.\n\nIt’s just… the wound-care charts, there’s a pattern in them. I know management doesn’t want people seeing it. My supervisor says it’s just late entries or coding mistakes, and I try to take that in, I really do, but it doesn’t feel like that to me.\n\nIt feels like they’re watching what I write because I’m the one who caught it. Does that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe you have a special role or mission at work that others don't understand?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a “mission”… that sounds a little big, and I’m not trying to make myself sound special.\n\nBut yes, I do feel like I noticed something in the wound-care charts that other people aren’t taking seriously. There’s a pattern there, and when management says it’s just late entries or coding mistakes, I try to hear that, I really do… but it doesn’t feel right.\n\nIt feels like they don’t want anyone to see it, and like they’re watching what I write because I’m the one who caught it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have special insights or abilities at work that set you apart from your colleagues?\n\nPatient: Maybe… yes, a little. I don’t mean like I’m better than the other aides or nurses. They’re good at their jobs.\n\nIt’s just with those wound-care charts, I can see a pattern that other people keep brushing off. My supervisor says it’s late entries or coding mistakes, and I try to accept that, I really do. But it feels too connected to just be mistakes.\n\nAnd I know this may sound odd, but it feels like management doesn’t want anyone to notice it… and like they’re watching what I write because I’m the one who caught it. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your ability to see patterns in the wound-care charts makes you more important or special compared to others at work?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it makes me more important than anyone else. I don’t want to come across like that. Everyone works hard, and I know I’m just one aide there.\n\nBut… I do feel like I’m the one who saw it. The wound-care charts are showing a pattern, and management doesn’t want anyone to see it. My supervisor keeps saying it’s just late entries or coding mistakes, and I try to accept that because I don’t want to be difficult.\n\nBut I can tell they’re watching what I write now, because I’m the one who caught it. I know that sounds like a lot… I’m just trying to explain it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think others would see the same pattern in the wound-care charts if they looked closely?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, if they really looked closely, they would see it too. I’m not saying I’m the only person who could ever notice it or anything like that.\n\nIt’s just that once you see how the wound-care notes line up, it’s hard to unsee. I just… I know nobody else seems to hear it, so I’m trying not to make too much of it.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices ever tell you to do something or take a specific action?\n\nPatient: Not really like… “go do this now,” no. I mean, I’ve been stressed and a little embarrassed about all this, but I wouldn’t say I’ve been feeling sad exactly. I don’t know if I’d call them disconnected exactly, but they move too fast and jump around. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you felt extremely energetic or irritable and didn't need much sleep?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, there are days where I feel like I’m finally awake, like I can see what needs to be fixed at work and I’m supposed to do something about it.\n\nI don’t always think of it as a bad thing at first. It feels productive, kind of important. But if my husband, or one of the nurses, tells me to slow down, I can get really irritated… I snap more than I should, like they’re getting in the way of something that matters. I know that probably doesn’t sound great.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voices are real or do you think they might be a product of your own mind?\n\nPatient: I… I want to say maybe it’s stress or my mind trying to make sense of things, because I know that sounds more reasonable. It’s clear, like somebody is standing right next to me saying it. And then the unit will be quiet, and nobody else seems to hear anything, so… I know that makes me sound… I don’t know.\n\nI guess part of me thinks it must be my mind, because no one else hears it. But in the moment, it doesn’t feel like it’s coming from me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely energetic or irritable that lasted for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: Yes… it has lasted more than just a day sometimes. I try not to make too much of it because at first it feels like I’m just being useful, like I’m finally awake and seeing what needs to be fixed at work.\n\nBut then if my husband, or one of the nurses, tells me to slow down, I can get… sharper than I mean to. I snap at them, like they’re blocking something important. I don’t like admitting that, because I know it doesn’t sound very good.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel irritable most of the time or just during certain periods?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s all the time, no. And then if my husband or one of the nurses tells me to slow down, I can snap at them. Like they’re getting in the way of something important.\n\nI know that probably sounds harsh. I don’t mean to be that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voices or messages you're hearing are specifically about you or your role at work?\n\nPatient: I… yes, I guess it does feel like it’s about my role at work. Not in a dramatic way, I don’t mean to sound important or anything.\n\nIt’s just… the wound-care charts, there’s a pattern in them. I know management doesn’t want people seeing it. My supervisor says it’s just late entries or coding mistakes, and I try to take that in, I really do, but it doesn’t feel like that to me.\n\nIt feels like they’re watching what I write because I’m the one who caught it. Does that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe you have a special role or mission at work that others don't understand?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a “mission”… that sounds a little big, and I’m not trying to make myself sound special.\n\nBut yes, I do feel like I noticed something in the wound-care charts that other people aren’t taking seriously. There’s a pattern there, and when management says it’s just late entries or coding mistakes, I try to hear that, I really do… but it doesn’t feel right.\n\nIt feels like they don’t want anyone to see it, and like they’re watching what I write because I’m the one who caught it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have special insights or abilities at work that set you apart from your colleagues?\n\nPatient: Maybe… yes, a little. I don’t mean like I’m better than the other aides or nurses. They’re good at their jobs.\n\nIt’s just with those wound-care charts, I can see a pattern that other people keep brushing off. My supervisor says it’s late entries or coding mistakes, and I try to accept that, I really do. But it feels too connected to just be mistakes.\n\nAnd I know this may sound odd, but it feels like management doesn’t want anyone to notice it… and like they’re watching what I write because I’m the one who caught it. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your ability to see patterns in the wound-care charts makes you more important or special compared to others at work?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it makes me more important than anyone else. I don’t want to come across like that. Everyone works hard, and I know I’m just one aide there.\n\nBut… I do feel like I’m the one who saw it. The wound-care charts are showing a pattern, and management doesn’t want anyone to see it. My supervisor keeps saying it’s just late entries or coding mistakes, and I try to accept that because I don’t want to be difficult.\n\nBut I can tell they’re watching what I write now, because I’m the one who caught it. I know that sounds like a lot… I’m just trying to explain it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think others would see the same pattern in the wound-care charts if they looked closely?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, if they really looked closely, they would see it too. I’m not saying I’m the only person who could ever notice it or anything like that.\n\nIt’s just that once you see how the wound-care notes line up, it’s hard to unsee. My supervisor keeps saying it’s late entries or coding mistakes, and I’m trying not to be stubborn about it, but it doesn’t feel like that to me.\n\nAnd I know this probably sounds suspicious, but it feels like management doesn’t want people looking too closely. Since I brought it up, I can tell they’re paying attention to what I write. I don’t know if I’d call it “special,” exactly… I don’t want to sound like I think I’m important or anything.

I don’t like admitting that, because I know it doesn’t sound very good.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel irritable most of the time or just during certain periods?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s all the time, no. Most of the time I’m… I try to be pretty even, or at least I hope I am.\n\nIt’s more like certain periods, certain days, where I feel like I’m finally awake and I can see everything that needs fixing at work. And then if my husband or one of the nurses tells me to slow down, I can snap at them. Like they’re getting in the way of something important.\n\nI know that probably sounds harsh. I don’t mean to be that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voices or messages you're hearing are specifically about you or your role at work?\n\nPatient: I… yes, I guess it does feel like it’s about my role at work. Not in a dramatic way, I don’t mean to sound important or anything.\n\nIt’s just… the wound-care charts, there’s a pattern in them. I know management doesn’t want people seeing it. My supervisor says it’s just late entries or coding mistakes, and I try to take that in, I really do, but it doesn’t feel like that to me.\n\nIt feels like they’re watching what I write because I’m the one who caught it. Does that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe you have a special role or mission at work that others don't understand?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a “mission”… that sounds a little big, and I’m not trying to make myself sound special.\n\nBut yes, I do feel like I noticed something in the wound-care charts that other people aren’t taking seriously. There’s a pattern there, and when management says it’s just late entries or coding mistakes, I try to hear that, I really do… but it doesn’t feel right.\n\nIt feels like they don’t want anyone to see it, and like they’re watching what I write because I’m the one who caught it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have special insights or abilities at work that set you apart from your colleagues?\n\nPatient: Maybe… yes, a little. I don’t mean like I’m better than the other aides or nurses. They’re good at their jobs.\n\nIt’s just with those wound-care charts, I can see a pattern that other people keep brushing off. My supervisor says it’s late entries or coding mistakes, and I try to accept that, I really do. But it feels too connected to just be mistakes.\n\nAnd I know this may sound odd, but it feels like management doesn’t want anyone to notice it… and like they’re watching what I write because I’m the one who caught it. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your ability to see patterns in the wound-care charts makes you more important or special compared to others at work?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it makes me more important than anyone else. I don’t want to come across like that. Everyone works hard, and I know I’m just one aide there.\n\nBut… I do feel like I’m the one who saw it. The wound-care charts are showing a pattern, and management doesn’t want anyone to see it. My supervisor keeps saying it’s just late entries or coding mistakes, and I try to accept that because I don’t want to be difficult.\n\nBut I can tell they’re watching what I write now, because I’m the one who caught it. I know that sounds like a lot… I’m just trying to explain it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think others would see the same pattern in the wound-care charts if they looked closely?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, if they really looked closely, they would see it too. I’m not saying I’m better or more capable overall. I’m just trying to do my job right, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you recently experienced a period where you felt extremely happy or euphoric?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “euphoric” exactly… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nBut there are some days where I feel like I’m finally awake, like I can see what needs to be fixed at work and I’m supposed to do something about it. But it doesn’t feel like that’s all it is.\n\nAnd since I pointed it out, I can’t shake the feeling that management knows I caught it… like they’re watching what I write now. I know that may sound like a lot, but that’s how it feels to me.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had a period where you felt extremely happy or euphoric that lasted for several days or longer?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 50 / nursing_assistant / some_college\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Elevated_Irritable_Mood\nmanifestation: \"Some days I feel like I'm finally awake and meant to fix everything at work, but if my husband or a nurse tells me to slow down I snap at them like they're blocking something important.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if I’d say “extremely happy” for several days… that sounds a little strong.

There are days, though, where I feel like I’m finally awake, like I can see exactly what needs to be fixed at work and I’m meant to do something about it. It feels good, like I have a purpose. But then if my husband or one of the nurses tells me to slow down, I get irritated really quickly and snap, like they’re blocking something important. I know that probably doesn’t sound good, but that’s what it feels like.

  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n20\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF23, F25.1, F31.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well... I’m a little embarrassed to say it, because I know it may sound strange, but I’ve been hearing voices at work. It’s like they’re coming from the call-light speaker, or sometimes the hallway vents, telling me which patient records have been changed. It sounds very clear, like someone standing right beside me, even when the unit is quiet... and nobody else seems to hear it. I just thought I should come in and make sure I’m handling it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to understand what the voices are telling you, or does it make perfect sense to you?\n\nPatient: It makes sense to me, mostly. I mean… I know it probably sounds odd, but the voices are very clear, like someone right next to me, not muffled or anything. They’ll come through the call-light speaker or the hallway vents and tell me which patient records have been changed. I don’t really have trouble understanding the words. I just… I know nobody else seems to hear it, so I’m trying not to make too much of it.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices ever tell you to do something or take a specific action?\n\nPatient: Not really like… “go do this now,” no. It’s more that they tell me which patient records have been changed. Like it comes through the call-light speaker or the vents, very clear, and I can understand it. I know that probably sounds strange, but it’s not like they’re ordering me around exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really depressed, no. I mean, I’ve been stressed and a little embarrassed about all this, but I wouldn’t say I’ve been feeling sad exactly. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to keep your thoughts straight or express them in a way that feels coherent to you?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… but I don’t want to overstate it. It’s more like my mind keeps jumping around — from wound measurements, to patient initials, to dates on the forms, to what it all means — like I have ten tabs open at once and none of them will close.  \n\nI can usually explain it if I slow down, I think… I hope I’m making sense right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your thoughts are disconnected from each other in a way that feels unusual or difficult for you to manage?\n\nPatient: Yes… somewhat. I don’t know if I’d call them disconnected exactly, but they move too fast and jump around. Like I’ll be thinking about wound measurements, then patient initials, then dates on the forms, then what it all means, and it’s like I’ve got ten tabs open in my head and none of them will close.\n\nI can usually follow it if I really slow myself down, but it does feel hard to manage sometimes. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you felt extremely energetic or irritable and didn't need much sleep?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, there are days where I feel like I’m finally awake, like I can see what needs to be fixed at work and I’m supposed to do something about it.\n\nI don’t always think of it as a bad thing at first. It feels productive, kind of important. But if my husband, or one of the nurses, tells me to slow down, I can get really irritated… I snap more than I should, like they’re getting in the way of something that matters. I know that probably doesn’t sound great.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voices are real or do you think they might be a product of your own mind?\n\nPatient: I… I want to say maybe it’s stress or my mind trying to make sense of things, because I know that sounds more reasonable. But when it happens, it feels real. Really real.\n\nI hear it from the call-light speaker sometimes, or from the hallway vents, telling me which patient records have been changed. It’s clear, like somebody is standing right next to me saying it. And then the unit will be quiet, and nobody else seems to hear anything, so… I know that makes me sound… I don’t know.\n\nI guess part of me thinks it must be my mind, because no one else hears it. But in the moment, it doesn’t feel like it’s coming from me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely energetic or irritable that lasted for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: Yes… it has lasted more than just a day sometimes. I try not to make too much of it because at first it feels like I’m just being useful, like I’m finally awake and seeing what needs to be fixed at work.\n\nBut then if my husband, or one of the nurses, tells me to slow down, I can get… sharper than I mean to. I snap at them, like they’re blocking something important. I don’t like admitting that, because I know it doesn’t sound very good.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel irritable most of the time or just during certain periods?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s all the time, no. Most of the time I’m… I try to be pretty even, or at least I hope I am.\n\nIt’s more like certain periods, certain days, where I feel like I’m finally awake and I can see everything that needs fixing at work. And then if my husband or one of the nurses tells me to slow down, I can snap at them. Like they’re getting in the way of something important.\n\nI know that probably sounds harsh. I don’t mean to be that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voices or messages you're hearing are specifically about you or your role at work?\n\nPatient: I… yes, I guess it does feel like it’s about my role at work. Not in a dramatic way, I don’t mean to sound important or anything.\n\nIt’s just… the wound-care charts, there’s a pattern in them. I know management doesn’t want people seeing it. My supervisor says it’s just late entries or coding mistakes, and I try to take that in, I really do, but it doesn’t feel like that to me.\n\nIt feels like they’re watching what I write because I’m the one who caught it. Does that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe you have a special role or mission at work that others don't understand?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a “mission”… that sounds a little big, and I’m not trying to make myself sound special.\n\nBut yes, I do feel like I noticed something in the wound-care charts that other people aren’t taking seriously. There’s a pattern there, and when management says it’s just late entries or coding mistakes, I try to hear that, I really do… but it doesn’t feel right.\n\nIt feels like they don’t want anyone to see it, and like they’re watching what I write because I’m the one who caught it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have special insights or abilities at work that set you apart from your colleagues?\n\nPatient: Maybe… yes, a little. I don’t mean like I’m better than the other aides or nurses. They’re good at their jobs.\n\nIt’s just with those wound-care charts, I can see a pattern that other people keep brushing off. My supervisor says it’s late entries or coding mistakes, and I try to accept that, I really do. But it feels too connected to just be mistakes.\n\nAnd I know this may sound odd, but it feels like management doesn’t want anyone to notice it… and like they’re watching what I write because I’m the one who caught it. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your ability to see patterns in the wound-care charts makes you more important or special compared to others at work?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it makes me more important than anyone else. I don’t want to come across like that. Everyone works hard, and I know I’m just one aide there.\n\nBut… I do feel like I’m the one who saw it. The wound-care charts are showing a pattern, and management doesn’t want anyone to see it. My supervisor keeps saying it’s just late entries or coding mistakes, and I try to accept that because I don’t want to be difficult.\n\nBut I can tell they’re watching what I write now, because I’m the one who caught it. I know that sounds like a lot… I’m just trying to explain it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think others would see the same pattern in the wound-care charts if they looked closely?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, if they really looked closely, they would see it too. I’m not saying I’m the only person who could ever notice it or anything like that.\n\nIt’s just that once you see how the wound-care notes line up, it’s hard to unsee. My supervisor keeps saying it’s late entries or coding mistakes, and I’m trying not to be stubborn about it, but it doesn’t feel like that to me.\n\nAnd I know this probably sounds suspicious, but it feels like management doesn’t want people looking too closely. Since I brought it up, I can tell they’re paying attention to what I write. I hope that makes sense… I’m not trying to sound dramatic.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your ability to see patterns in the wound-care charts makes you better than others at your job?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think it makes me better than anyone. I really don’t want you to think I’m saying that.\n\nI just feel like I noticed something important in the wound-care charts that other people are explaining away. My supervisor says it’s late entries or coding mistakes, and I know that could sound reasonable, but to me the pattern is there. And since I brought it up, it feels like management is paying attention to what I write, like they know I caught it.\n\nI’m not trying to make myself sound special. I’m just… I don’t know how else to explain what I’m seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're more intelligent or capable than your colleagues at work?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’m more intelligent than them. I mean, some people at work have been there longer than me, and they know a lot.\n\nI guess I feel like I’m careful with details, maybe more careful than some people with the charts, but I don’t want that to sound like I’m putting anyone down. I’m not saying I’m better or more capable overall. I’m just trying to do my job right, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you recently experienced a period where you felt extremely happy or euphoric?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “euphoric” exactly… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nBut there are some days where I feel like I’m finally awake, like I can see what needs to be fixed at work and I’m supposed to do something about it. It feels good in the moment, like I have a purpose.\n\nAnd then if my husband, or one of the nurses, tells me to slow down, I get irritated really fast. I snap at them, which I’m not proud of. It just feels like they’re getting in the way of something important, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have a special or unique role in uncovering the truth about the pattern in the wound-care charts?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it “special,” exactly… I don’t want to sound like I think I’m important or anything.\n\nIt’s more like… I feel like I’m the one who noticed it.
